one key for London, Stockholm and Nijmegen · the Balbases 1677 table

London and Nijmegen → Copenhagen · syllabic nomenclator · 1676–78

Bernardo de Salinas and Pedro Ronquillo to Baltasar de Fuenmayor, 1676–78

Archives générales du Royaume, Brussels, Secrétairerie d’État et de Guerre, inv. nr. 2559 · DECODE R966–R984 and R1001, twenty letters · sixteen with a contemporary decipherment in the margin, four without

The Balbases key applies unchanged to all twenty: 88% of 23,463 groups get a value, including the two letters that DECODE lists with no decipherment; eighteen carry a contemporary decipherment in the margin, and with it all twenty now build sentences and are translated, with a few holes

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 21 September 2026 · updated 22 October 2026

Method: not solved · Extent: text not obtained

Credits · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used)

not solved
On this pageThe lettersThe four letters without a marginMethodWhat remains uncertainFull textsSources
Summary. Fuenmayor’s papers in Brussels hold ciphered letters from four Spanish envoys. The key for Balbases was rebuilt in this project from his margins, and it then decrypted Hernán Núñez’s Stockholm letters unchanged. It was then applied to the other ciphered letters in the volume: eight letters from Bernardo de Salinas in London (August 1676 – March 1677) and twelve from Pedro Ronquillo at the Nijmegen congress (December 1676 – March 1678). It fits all twenty and gives values for 88.4% of the 23,463 groups in DECODE’s transcriptions; the residue is transcription noise. Eighteen letters have a contemporary decipherment in the margin, which the key output confirms; for two of them (R970, R973) DECODE recorded the margin only in a comment. Two have none: Ronquillo of 26 February and 19 March 1678. DECODE lists the last two as “non-decrypted”, that is, with no decipherment attached. No decipherment of these two was found on DECODE or in the margins; they were deciphered here on the images with the key. Most of the work was on DECODE’s transcriptions: six are typed with no group boundaries at all, and had to be divided into groups against the key’s vocabulary. Carpio in Rome used a different cipher; the other northern envoys shared this one. Extent: partial. The key output alone is unspaced, with a doubtful or unread group every few words, and the reading audit of 22 October 2026 set all twenty letters back to decoded, not read. The same day each margin was transcribed from the DECODE images and aligned word by word with the key decode, cut into words page by page; the two unmargined letters were re-transcribed from the images and deciphered with the key. All twenty letters now build sentences and are translated (read/R*_reading.md, _en.md), in the writers’ spelling restored from the key decode (23 October). About 60 code values from the margins were added to the key. Holes remain in the faded first page of R983, in about a dozen code groups each of R982 and R984 that no margin glosses, and in single words elsewhere.

02 Four letters at first taken as unmargined

R982 and R984 have no margin and were deciphered on the images with the key. R970 and R973 do have a margin, which DECODE recorded only in a comment; their readings follow it. Quotations are from the readings of 22–23 October 2026.

Ronquillo, Nijmegen, 26 February 1678 (R984)

Ronquillo replies to Fuenmayor’s reports on the assembly of the northern allies. Everything depends on the resolutions of the English parliament, and on whether Charles II will really break with France. Ronquillo, who knows that court, doubts it: [?no] me atrevere a hacer todavia juicio fijo ni de la paz ni de la guerra mientras no tuvieremos apariencias mas ciertas y seguras de las deliveraciones de Su Magestad Britanica. The Danes complain about subsidy remittances and say they can do nothing. The Elector of Brandenburg is in Pomerania. At the end, the Duke of Hanover’s intrigue, "esta trama", runs through his kinship with Madame.

Ronquillo, Nijmegen, 19 March 1678 (R982)

This comes ten days after the fall of Ghent. DECODE’s transcription is poor (full of "?"); re-transcribed from the soft scan, the letter reads with holes: the congress, Flanders taken by surprise, the two months that are lacking, the Brunswick princes, Con que de todas maneras estamos muy mal, and Ronquillo’s own melancolicas reflexiones.

Salinas, London, 15 September 1676 (R970)

Ya V.S. se hallara noticioso del fatal suceso de Mastrique: the fall of Maastricht, “quiso la fortuna cerrarnos esta puerta”. The discourses at the English court turn towards a peace, and the "progresos" on Fuenmayor’s side weigh on them. The minister has taken the news calmly. There is mention of the Danish fleet.

Salinas, London, 2 March 1677 (R973)

The English have made a beginning, no siendo tan malos principios quando los dan en negocio que tanto nos importa como retirar las tropas de Francia, en que me prometo persistiran. The preparations of recent months show that everything is in earnest. Talk treats the peace as good as made.

03 Method

  1. The Balbases key was used, with the four values added for Hernán Núñez (167 Suecia, 161 resolución, os para, 6+ o). It was applied without changes to DECODE’s transcriptions of all twenty records.
  2. Six records (R971, R974, R978, R979, R983, R1001) are typed character by character with no group boundaries, and at first gave 0%. Each line was rejoined and divided into groups by dynamic programming over the key’s vocabulary: fewest groups, with one-character groups penalised. They then decrypted at 90–94%.
  3. R970 and R973 write one sign as "Infinity" (∞), which is c before a vowel. R970’s transcriber also writes 5 where the key has a and b for que. These conventions account for most of its groups without a value.
  4. The margins were compared with the key output. They agree.
  5. Re-reading, 22–23 October 2026: every margin transcribed from full-resolution crops of the DECODE images (DECODE’s margin transcriptions checked against them) and aligned word by word with the key decode; the decode cut into words page by page (spaced.py); R982 and R984, and the transcriptions of R966–R970, R973, R976 and R977, corrected on the images (txi/). About 60 code values from the margins entered the key (keyx.py; coverage 93.6% to 95.4%). The readings first written in modern spelling were restored to the writers’ own from the key decode (tomavan, assi, haver, Magestad), with no word changed.

04 What remains uncertain

  • R983, the first page: the margin is faded almost to nothing on the DECODE scan, and groups l, c, 1q, L2, pA, 11 and 300 are not in the key, so its first passage has many holes.
  • R982 and R984, without a margin: the opening groups of R982 and about a dozen phrase-level holes, and about a dozen code groups in R984 (138, 91, 113, 460, 600, 109 and others) that no margined letter glosses. R982’s scan is soft.
  • Single words in R966, R967, R968, R971/R972, R976, R977, R979, R981 and R1001, where the margin is faded or blotted and the figures alone do not settle the word.
  • Grades: A for R966, R968–R975, R978–R981 and R1001; B for R967, R976, R977, R982, R983 and R984.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

R966, Salinas, 21 Aug 1676 decoded, not read

Salinas (London) to Baltasar de Fuenmayor, 21 Aug 1676: deciphered with the Balbases 1677 key unchanged (87.7% of 628 groups); the file also gives DECODE's transcription of the contemporary margin decipherment, which the key output agrees with.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R982, Ronquillo, 19 Mar 1678 decoded, not read

Ronquillo (Nijmegen) to Baltasar de Fuenmayor, 19 Mar 1678; no contemporary decipherment in DECODE's transcription, a new reading with the Balbases 1677 key (71.2% of 813 groups). Ten days after the fall of Ghent: the congress, Flanders surprised, the Brunswick princes, 'estamos muy mal', 'melancólicas reflexiones'. Read for gist only (DECODE's transcription is the worst of the set).

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R984, Ronquillo, 26 Feb 1678 decoded, not read

Ronquillo (Nijmegen) to Baltasar de Fuenmayor, 26 Feb 1678; no contemporary decipherment in DECODE's transcription, a new reading with the Balbases 1677 key (86.0% of 1144 groups). On Fuenmayor's reports of the assembly of the northern allies: everything hangs on the English parliament and whether the King really breaks with France; Danish complaints about subsidies; Brandenburg in Pomerania; the Duke of Hanover's intrigue through his kinship with Madame.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription
